Every little thing pulls my attention away. Like, a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm sounds, or I just hear someone moving a pallet, and suddenly I've completely lost track of what I was doing. I get pulled off my count, and the next thing I know, I realize I've put the completely wrong numbers into the inventory. It's really starting to mess up my work.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, especially after my shifts. Usually, I'd be completely worn out from being on my feet at work, but lately... I come home and I'm just buzzing. \n\nSitting on the couch to relax just feels impossible right now. So instead of resting, I end up reorganizing the kitchen cabinets, or I'll go out and start sorting through all the storage bins in the garage. I just can't seem to just sit and be still.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have been doing that lately. For example, I've been going to these craft markets and... well, I spent way more than I should have. I just kept buying paints, frames, and tools for all these projects I was absolutely sure would turn into something useful. I didn't even stop to think about the cost at the time. And now... honestly, I'm just really embarrassed looking at my credit card bill.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Especially with my husband. We've been arguing quite a bit lately... enough that it really just feels like we're walking around each other right now to avoid a fight. He recently retired, and we were supposed to be settling into that, but instead it's just been tense. \n\nAnd honestly, it's not just at home. It's affecting my job, too. Like I mentioned earlier with the inventory errors... it's gotten bad enough that I've had to start double-checking things with my supervisor just to make sure I didn't mess up the counts. It just feels like it's putting a strain on everything.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or behaving when you were a child?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I was a pretty typical kid, really. I never got in trouble at school for acting out or... or not staying in my seat, anything like that. So, no, this whole feeling of not being able to sit still, it's... it's really just a recent thing for me. I definitely didn't have that problem growing up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these problems with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity are significantly interfering with your daily life or causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they really are. I mean, between the tension with my husband and having to get my boss to check my work... it's just stressful. And the credit card bill from all that craft stuff is definitely causing me distress. So, yes, it's definitely interfering with my life right now.\n\nDoctor: Did you exhibit high levels of physical activity as a child, such as running, climbing, or being unable to play quietly?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I said, I was a pretty normal kid. I liked to play outside and ride my bike, but I wasn't... I wasn't bouncing off the walls or climbing on things I shouldn't be. I could play quietly just fine, read a book, that sort of thing. I was never one of those kids who couldn't settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods in your life where you didn't have these problems with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Oh, yeah, definitely. Like I was saying earlier, this is all pretty new for me. For most of my life, I didn't have any issues with focusing or feeling restless. I could just go to work—I move freight, so you really have to pay attention to what you're doing—and I never had a problem staying on task or getting things done. \n\nIt's really just been lately that all this started happening. Basically my whole life up until recently, I didn't have these problems at all.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms of inattention, restlessness, and impulsivity been constant since they started, or have they come and gone?\n\nPatient: They've been pretty constant. It's... well, this has all been going on for about a month now. It basically started right around the time my husband stopped working outside the home. Our usual routine changed completely, and ever since then, these issues haven't really let up at all.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when you first started noticing these problems with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm 57 now, so... 57. Like I said, it just started about a month ago. I never had any of this before then.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for at least six months?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. Like I just mentioned, it's only been going on for about a month. Before that, I didn't have any of these problems at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any other mental health issues, such as depression or anxiety, around the same time these symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't been feeling depressed or anxious or anything like that. It's just the trouble focusing and the restlessness that started up this past month.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods where these symptoms of inattention, restlessness, and impulsivity went away for a while since they started?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't gone away. It's just been a constant thing for the whole month since it started. I haven't really had a break from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant stressors or life changes around the time these symptoms started, besides your husband's retirement?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, it's just been his retirement. Everything else has been pretty much exactly the same. Work is the same as always, and nothing else big has happened at home or anything. So, yeah, just that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you didn't experience these symptoms of inattention, restlessness, and impulsivity for at least a few days since they started?\n\nPatient: No, like I was just saying, it's been pretty constant. I haven't really had a break from it at all. It hasn't let up for even a day or two since it started.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any manic episodes or extreme mood elevations since these symptoms started?\n\nPatient: I don't know about extreme, but... some days I do feel almost too upbeat. Like, I'll feel like I can fix up the whole house and get everything sorted out at work before dinner. But then, if my husband questions one of my plans, I just snap at him over something really small. So my mood definitely shifts like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other significant medical conditions or taken any new medications around the time these symptoms started?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't been sick with anything else, and I'm not taking any new medications. I'm just always feeling like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had these symptoms for less than 6 months as you mentioned earlier, is that correct?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely been less than six months.
